Title: Vita Germani Episcopi Autissiodorensis (Life of Germanus, Bishop of Auxerre)
Creator: Constantius of Lyon (5th century) -- Latin critical text, ed. Wilhelm Levison
Publisher: Monumenta Germaniae Historica (MGH), Scriptores rerum Merovingicarum, tomus VII (Passiones vitaeque sanctorum aevi Merovingici, cum supplemento et appendice), pp. 225-283
Publication Date: 1920
Rights: Public Domain
Rights basis: "Public Domain Mark 1.0" per archive.org; 1920 publication, long out of US copyright.
Source: archive.org item `passionesvitaequesanctorum5`, OCR text (DjVuTXT), downloaded directly 2026-09-09. Extracted from the full volume, a 942-page collection of many Merovingian-era and adjacent saints' lives (Vita Germani sits between Venantius Fortunatus's Vita Severini and the Vita Lupi Trecensis in this tome) -- vendoring the whole volume was judged disproportionate to what this world's Registry actually needs.
Language: lat
Provenance note: this header was prepended by the build thread. Boundaries: extracted from the section title "VITA GERMANI / EPISCOPI AUTISSIODORENSIS / AUCTORE CONSTANTIO" (p. 225, Levison's own Prolegomena to the edition) through "EXPLICIT VITA SANCTI GERMANI EPISCOPI" (p. 283) -- Levison's own citation for this work (pp. 225-283) confirmed directly against the volume's own running-header pagination. This includes Levison's scholarly Prolegomena (manuscript tradition, Constantius's own biography and relationship to Sidonius Apollinaris, the cult of Germanus) as well as the edited ancient text itself (the two dedicatory letters to bishops Patiens and Censurius, the capitula, and the narrative in full) -- the same whole-unit standard applied to the companion Eucherius/Hilary of Arles extracts from Migne PL 50, same session. A second archive.org djvu.txt copy was not cross-checked for completeness (contrast the Salvian file's own provenance note, where a genuine OCR gap was caught this way) -- this is a single-copy vendoring, not independently corroborated against a second scan. OCR quality is the roughest of the six files vendored this session: MGH's critical-edition apparatus criticus (superscript sigla, interlinear variant readings in smaller type) is heavily garbled by page-level OCR, considerably more than the CSEL 21 or Salvian volumes' plainer page layout. Preserved as scanned, per this project's standing practice -- but flagged here explicitly as the file needing the most caution before any specific wording is quoted from it; verify any load-bearing claim against the page images before treating a specific Latin phrase as certain.
Content note: no public-domain English translation of this text exists (the only English translation, F. R. Hoare's "The Western Fathers," Sheed and Ward, 1954, is access-restricted on every archive.org copy checked and not confirmed public domain) -- this Latin critical edition is the acquisition target, pending a translation effort, same as Faustus of Riez's De Gratia. Constantius's Vita Germani is the primary source for Germanus of Auxerre's two documented visits to Britain (429 and c. 435-437) to combat Pelagianism there. Vendored for Gallic Monastic-Ascetic Christianity (era 2, c. 360-450) as a newly-surfaced lead, NOT YET Boundary-Checked against this world's own window (Germanus's own see, Auxerre, is a looser geographic fit than Marseilles/Lerins/Tours) -- see gallic_G1_Scope_and_Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md's 2026-09-09 addendum; this is not yet a Source Registry row.

---
